Renormalization of the Einstein-Cartan Theory in First-Order Form
F. T. Brandt, J. Frenkel, S. Martins-Filho, D. G. C. McKeon

Abstract
We examine the Einstein-Cartan (EC) theory in first-order form, which has a diffeomorphism as well as a local Lorentz invariance. We study the renormalizability of this theory in the framework of the Batalin-Vilkovisky formalism, which allows for a gauge invariant renormalization. Using the background field method, we discuss the gauge invariance of the background effective action and analyze the Ward identities which reflect the symmetries of the EC theory. As an application, we compute, in a general background gauge, the self-energy of the tetrad field at one-loop order.



I
Introduction

The EC theory is an extension of general relativity that describes a spacetime with curvature and torsion, arising from the spin of elementary particles. Due to this feature, the EC theory may solve the Big Bang singularity problem that occurs in cosmological models based on general relativity (GR)
[
1
,
2
]
. In GR, the fundamental entity is the metric
g_{\mu\nu}(x)

[
3
]
. The EC theory differs from GR by being formulated in a Riemann-Cartan geometry that encodes, in addition to diffeomorphism, local Lorentz symmetry. It may be regarded as a gauge theory consisting of local translations and local Lorentz transformations
[
4
,
5
,
6
]
. The first-order form of the EC theory involves a tetrad field
\mathchoice{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}(x)
as well as an independent spin-connection field
\omega_{\mu ab}(x)
. These are gauge fields that implement the diffeomorphism and the local Lorentz invariance of this theory. In a previous paper, the quantization of the EC theory in first-order form has been systematically discussed
[
7
]
. It was shown that the gauge algebra for both these gauge transformations is closed, and the BRST invariance of this theory was established.

The purpose of this paper is to show that this theory is renormalizable in the sense that the ultraviolet divergences are controlled by the gauge symmetries, so that there are counterterms available to cancel all such divergences. To this end, we will employ the Batalin-Vilkovisky (BV) formalism
[
8
,
9
]
, which is useful for maintaining gauge invariance during the renormalization process. We shall also use the background field method
[
10
,
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
,
15
]
, which is convenient for preserving the gauge symmetries of the divergent part of the effective action of the EC theory in first-order form.

The paper is organized as follows. In Sec. II, we reproduce some properties of the EC theory in first-order form together with the background field method. In Sec. III, we formulate this theory in the framework of the BV formalism. In Sec. IV, we apply this approach to derive the Zinn-Justin equation
[
16
]
for the effective action and to provide a recursive proof of the renormalizability of this theory. Using this approach, all subdivergences at any order of the perturbative expansion are eliminated through the renormalization procedure. In Sec. V, we examine the gauge invariance of the background effective action, which is applied to study the ultraviolet divergences of the tetrad and spin connection self-energies at one-loop order. A brief discussion of the results is presented in Sec. VI. Some relevant details of the calculations are given in the appendices.

II
EC theory in the background field gauge

In this section, we will review some results obtained in the first order form of the EC theory
[
7
]
, but will omit, for simplicity, the matter fields. The pure EC action involves the tetrad field

C^{\star ab}\left(-c^{\alpha}\omega_{\mu ab;\bar{\alpha}}-\omega_{\alpha ab}c^{\alpha}_{\ ;\bar{\mu}}\right)^{;\bar{\mu}}\\
&+C^{\star ab}\left[-C_{ab;\bar{\mu}}+\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega_{\mu pb}+\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega_{\mu ap}\right]^{;\bar{\mu}}.\end{split}

(12)

We note that the two pairs of ghosts,
(c_{\mu},{c}^{\star}_{\mu})
and
(C_{ab},{C}^{\star}_{ab})
, associated respectively with the diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations, generally couple among themselves. However, for the terms quadratic in the ghost fields, the two types of ghosts decouple. Moreover, as shown in the appendices, there occurs a significant simplification of the calculations for
\sigma=1/2
.

Setting
\epsilon_{\mu}=c_{\mu}\eta
and
\lambda_{ab}=C_{ab}\eta
, where
\eta
is an infinitesimal Grassmann quantity with ghost number
-1
, one may verify that the Faddeev-Popov Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}_{\text{FP}}=\mathcal{L}_{\text{EC}}+\bar{e}\mathcal{L}_{\text{gf}}+\bar{e}\mathcal{L}_{\text{gh}}

(13)

is invariant under the BRST transformations given in Eqs. (
9
) and (
10
) together with

\delta b_{\mu}=\delta B_{ab}=0,

(14)

\delta{c}^{\star}_{\mu}=-b_{\mu}\eta,\quad\delta{C}^{\star}_{ab}=-\frac{1}{\kappa}B_{ab}\eta;

(15)

and

\delta c_{\mu}=\kappa c^{\alpha}c_{\mu,\alpha}\eta,\quad\delta C_{ab}=\left(-\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}C_{kb}+\kappa c^{\alpha}C_{ab,\alpha}\right)\eta.

(16)

It has been shown in Ref.
[
7
]
that the full BRST generator

\delta=\delta_{\text{diff}}+\delta_{\text{LL}},

(17)

where
\delta_{\text{diff}}
and
\delta_{\text{LL}}
generate respectively diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations, is nilpotent due to the nilpotency of these generators as well as to the anticommutation relation

\{\delta_{\text{diff}},\delta_{\text{LL}}\}=0.

(18)

It is usual to define the BRST transformations of a functional
F
of the quantum fields by

\delta F=(sF)\eta,

(19)

where
s
is the BRST generator. We will use the notation
\mathop{\text{gh}}(F)
for the ghost number of
F
.
Since
\mathop{\text{gh}}(\eta)=-1
, it follows that the BRST generator
s
increases the ghost number of
F
by one unit, that is,
\mathop{\text{gh}}(sF)=\mathop{\text{gh}}(F)+1
.

III
BV formalism for the EC theory in the first-order form

The purpose of the BV formalism is to control gauge invariance during the perturbative renormalization of gauge theories. In the present case, such an approach must be adapted due the fact that we are dealing with two distinct gauge transformations.
To this end, it is convenient to use a compact notation where we denote the quantum fields
(\mathchoice{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}},\omega_{\mu ab})
by
(\phi_{i},\Phi_{I})
,
(b_{\mu},B_{ab})
by
(b_{i},B_{I})
,
(c_{\mu},{c}^{\star}_{\mu},C_{ab},{C}^{\star}_{ab})
by
(c_{i},{c}^{\star}_{i},C_{I},{C}^{\star}_{I})
, and the BRST transformations following from the gauge transformations (
9b
) and (
10b
) by

s\phi_{i}=r_{ij}(\phi)c_{j}+r_{iJ}(\phi)C_{J}

(20)

and

s\Phi_{I}=R_{Ij}(\Phi)c_{j}+R_{IJ}(\Phi)C_{J}.

(21)

Moreover, we have that
\mathop{\text{gh}}(\phi_{i},\Phi_{I})=\mathop{\text{gh}}(b_{i},B_{I})=0
,
\mathop{\text{gh}}(c_{i},C_{I})=-\mathop{\text{gh}}({c}^{\star}_{i},{C}^{\star}_{I})=1
.

We will next introduce a fermionic potential
\Psi
, which is a local functional of the quantum fields, with ghost number
-1
, as

\Psi=\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\bar{e}\left[{c}^{\star}_{i}\left(f_{ij}\phi_{j}-\frac{\xi}{2}b_{i}\right)+{C}^{\star}_{I}\left(F_{IJ}\Phi_{J}-\frac{\zeta}{2}B_{I}\right)\right],

(22)

where the gauge-fixing functions
f_{ij}
and
F_{IJ}
may be identified with the corresponding functions given in Eq. (
11
).

Then, using the relations (
20
), (
21
) and (
22
) together with the Eqs. (
14
), (
15
), (
16
), one may verify that the Faddeev-Popov action corresponding to (
13
), may be written in the simple form

\begin{split}S_{\text{FP}}={}&S_{\text{EC}}+s\Psi\\
={}&S_{\text{EC}}+\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\bar{e}\bigg\{-b_{i}\left(f_{ij}\phi_{j}-\frac{\xi}{2}b_{i}\right)-B_{I}\left(F_{IJ}\Phi_{J}-\frac{\zeta}{2}B_{I}\right)\\
&\quad+{c}^{\star}_{i}f_{ij}\left[r_{jk}(\phi)c_{k}+r_{jK}(\phi)C_{K}\right]+{C}^{\star}_{I}F_{IJ}\left]R_{Jk}(\Phi)c_{k}+R_{JK}(\Phi)C_{K}\right]\bigg\},\end{split}

(23)

which is equivalent to the one arising from the Eq. (
2
) and from the integration
\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\bar{e}
of Eqs. (
11
) and (
12
).

In the BV-formalism, it turns out to be convenient to introduce as well gauge-invariant antifields associated with all quantum fields of the theory
[
24
]
. Thus, if we denote the quantum fields in our theory:
\phi_{i},\Phi_{I},b_{i},B_{I},c_{i},{c}^{\star}_{i},C_{I},{C}^{\star}_{I}
collectively by
\chi_{n}
, one adds a new gauge-invariant action (due to the nilpotency of the BRST generator
s
) given by

\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\chi_{n}^{\ddagger}s\chi_{n}

(24)

which should have a zero ghost number. To ensure this, the antifield
\chi_{n}^{\ddagger}
must have a ghost number equal to
-\mathop{\text{gh}}(\chi_{n})-1
because the BRST generator
s
increases the ghost number by one unit. These antifields act as sources for the BRST transformations and are important to maintain the gauge invariance during renormalization.

Thus, the complete action

S_{\Psi}(\bar{\chi},\chi,\chi^{\ddagger})=S_{\text{EC}}+s\Psi+\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\chi_{n}^{\ddagger}s\chi_{n},

(25)

where the
\bar{\chi}
denotes background fields
\mathchoice{\bar{e}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{e}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{e}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{e}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
and
\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab}
,
will be invariant under the BRST transformations. This invariance may be expressed in the form

\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\frac{\delta_{r}S_{\Psi}}{\delta\chi_{n}}\frac{\delta_{l}S_{\Psi}}{\delta\chi_{n}^{\ddagger}}=\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\frac{\delta_{r}S_{\Psi}}{\delta\chi_{n}}s\chi_{n}=0,

(26)

where the
r
/
l
superscripts on the functional derivatives denote that these are taken from the right or the left, respectively.

A main element in the BV formalism is the introduction of an antibracket defined for two general functionals
F
and
G
as

(F,G)\equiv\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\left(\frac{\delta_{r}F}{\delta\chi_{n}}\frac{\delta_{l}G}{\delta\chi_{n}^{\ddagger}}-\frac{\delta_{r}F}{\delta\chi_{n}^{\ddagger}}\frac{\delta_{l}G}{\delta\chi_{n}}\right).

(27)

Replacing the functionals
F
and
G
by the bosonic action
S_{\Psi}
, one can easily verify that

\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\frac{\delta_{r}S_{\Psi}}{\delta\chi_{n}}\frac{\delta_{l}S_{\Psi}}{\delta\chi_{n}^{\ddagger}}=\frac{1}{2}(S_{\Psi},S_{\Psi}).

(28)

Thus, using the relation (
26
), one can see that this action satisfies the master equation

(S_{\Psi},S_{\Psi})=0

(29)

which expresses the BRST invariance of the action
S_{\Psi}(\bar{\chi},\chi,\chi^{\ddagger})
. It is important to mention that since the EC theory is of Yang-Mills type, the Faddeev-Popov gauge-fixed action coincides with the BV action.

IV
Gauge-invariant renormalization of EC theory

BRST invariance of the action leads to relations between the Green functions which are more directly expressed in terms of generating functionals. To this end we start from the functional:

Z(J,\bar{\chi},\chi^{\ddagger})=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi}\exp i\left(S_{\Psi}(\bar{\chi},\chi,\chi^{\ddagger})+\int\mathop{d^{4}x}J_{n}\chi_{n}\right).

(30)

Making a gauge transformation of the fields and using the gauge invariance of
Z
, leads to

J_{m}\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi}s\chi_{m}\exp i\left(S_{\Psi}(\bar{\chi},\chi,\chi^{\ddagger})+\int\mathop{d^{4}x}J_{n}\chi_{n}\right)=J_{m}\left\langle s\chi_{m}\right\rangle|_{\chi^{\ddagger},J}=0.

(31)

One now defines a generating functional for connected Green functions by

\exp iW(J,\bar{\chi},\chi^{\ddagger})=\frac{Z(J,\bar{\chi},\chi^{\ddagger})}{Z(0,0)}.

(32)

Then, the relation (
31
) can be written as

J_{m}\frac{\delta_{l}W}{\delta\chi_{m}^{\ddagger}}=0.

(33)

Next, one performs a Legendre transformation with respect to
J

\tilde{\chi}_{n}\equiv\langle\chi_{n}\rangle_{J,\tilde{\chi}^{\ddagger}}=\frac{\delta_{l}W}{\delta J_{n}}

(34)

and define the quantum effective action for one-particle irreducible Green functions as

\Gamma(\bar{\chi},\chi,\chi^{\ddagger})=W({J},\bar{\chi},{\chi}^{\ddagger})-\int\mathop{d^{4}x}{J}_{n}{\chi}_{n},

(35)

where we dropped, for simplicity of notation, the tilde denoting the mean fields and antifields. The fact that the effective action is defined by a Legendre transform implies the relations

\frac{\delta_{r}\Gamma}{\delta{\chi}_{n}}=-{J}_{n},\quad\frac{\delta_{l}\Gamma}{\delta{\chi}_{n}^{\ddagger}}=\frac{\delta_{l}W}{\delta{\chi}_{n}^{\ddagger}}.

(36)

Then, in terms of the effective action
\Gamma
, the identity (
33
) takes the form

\int\mathop{d^{4}x}\frac{\delta_{r}\Gamma}{\delta{\chi}_{n}}\frac{\delta_{l}\Gamma}{\delta{\chi}_{n}^{\ddagger}}=\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma,\Gamma)_{{\chi},{\chi}^{\ddagger}}=0.

(37)

This important relation is called the Zinn-Justin master equation
[
16
]
.

We now remark that the master equations (
29
) and (
37
) are given in terms of unrenormalized quantities. In the framework of BV approach, one must show that a similar result may be obtained if we replace them by renormalized quantities. Thus, the required equations take the form

(S_{\text{R}},S_{\text{R}})=0,\quad(\Gamma_{\text{R}},\Gamma_{\text{R}})=0.

(38)

BRST invariance is encoded in the above master equations. These are crucial to prove, order by order in a loop expansion, the renormalizability of the EC theory in first-order form. We note that BRST quantization involves local gauge transformations. Thus, in order to implement a BRST invariant renormalization, the counterterms must be local functions. Such a program works because the ultraviolet divergences, which occurs at very short distances, can be described by local functionals.
To evaluate these divergences, we will use dimensional regularization which preserves gauge invariance and henceforth follow the thorough discussion given in
[
25
]
(for the renormalization of a gauge-affine gravity, see ref.
[
26
]
).

Let us first consider the one-loop approximation for the effective action, which may be written as

\Gamma=\Gamma^{(1)}+O(\hbar^{2})=S+\hbar[\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{\text{fin}}^{(1)}]+O(\hbar^{2}),

(39)

where
\hbar
keeps track of the loop order in a perturbative expansion. Here,
S=S_{\Psi}(\bar{\chi},\chi,\chi^{\ddagger})
is the tree level action given in Eq. (
25
), and
\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}
,
\Gamma_{\text{fin}}^{(1)}
are respectively the divergent and finite parts of
\Gamma
in the one-loop approximation. This divergence fixes the form of the counterterm of the one-loop renormalized action
S_{\text{1R}}
, such that

S_{\text{1R}}=S-\hbar\Gamma^{(1)}_{\text{div}}.

(40)

Let us now consider

(S_{\text{1R}},S_{\text{1R}})=(S,S)-2\hbar(S,\Gamma^{(1)}_{\text{div}})+\hbar^{2}(\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)},\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}).

(41)

To evaluate this quantity, we substitute the expression (
39
) in the Eq. (
37
), which yields

\begin{split}(\Gamma,\Gamma)={}&(S,S)+2\hbar(S,\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)})+2\hbar(S,\Gamma_{\text{fin}}^{(1)})+O(\hbar^{2})\\
={}&2\hbar(S,\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)})+2\hbar(S,\Gamma_{\text{fin}}^{(1)})+O(\hbar^{2})=0,\end{split}

(42)

where we used the master equation (
29
). To first order in
\hbar
, we have a sum of two terms which must vanish independently, since one of them is infinite. Thus, we get

(S,\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)})=0.

(43)

Using this result in Eq. (
41
), together with Eq. (
29
), we obtain

(S_{\text{1R}},S_{\text{1R}})=\hbar^{2}(\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)},\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}).

(44)

Thus, we have shown that the renormalized action
S_{1R}
satisfies the master equation (
38
) up to terms of order
\hbar^{2}
.

The renormalized effective action to one loop order
\Gamma_{\text{1R}}
can now be constructed by replacing in Eq. (
39
),
S
by
S_{\text{1R}}
, which leads to the relation

\Gamma_{\text{1R}}=S_{\text{1R}}+\hbar[\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{\text{fin}}^{(1)}]=S+\hbar\Gamma_{\text{fin}}^{(1)}.

(45)

Thus,
\Gamma_{\text{1R}}
is finite at one-loop order and satisfies, by using Eq. (
44
), the relation

(\Gamma_{\text{1R}},\Gamma_{\text{1R}})=\hbar^{2}(\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)},\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}).

(46)

Let us now proceed by considering the two loop renormalized action
S_{\text{2R}}
defined by

S_{\text{2R}}=S_{\text{1R}}-\hbar^{2}\Gamma_{1,\text{div}}^{(2)}=S-\hbar\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}-\hbar^{2}\Gamma_{1,\text{div}}^{(2)},

(47)

where
\Gamma_{1,\text{div}}^{(2)}
is the divergent part at two-loop order constructed on the basis
S_{\text{1R}}
.
One can verify that this obeys the equation

(S,\Gamma_{1,\text{div}}^{(2)})=\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)},\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}).

(48)

Using this relation, we find that
S_{\text{2R}}
satisfies, up to terms of order
\hbar^{3}
, the master equation

(S_{\text{2R}},S_{\text{2R}})=2h^{3}(\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)},\Gamma_{1,\text{div}}^{(2)})+O(\hbar^{4}).

(49)

The effective action
\Gamma_{\text{2R}}
is obtained from
S_{\text{2R}}
by a similar procedure to that used earlier (compare with Eq. (
45
))

\begin{split}\Gamma_{\text{2R}}={}&S_{\text{2R}}+\hbar[\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)}+\Gamma^{(1)}_{\text{fin}}]+\hbar^{2}[\Gamma_{1,\text{div}}^{(2)}+\Gamma_{1,\text{fin}}^{(2)}]\\
={}&S+\hbar\Gamma_{\text{fin}}^{(1)}+\hbar^{2}\Gamma_{1,\text{fin}}^{(2)}\end{split}

(50)

and satisfies the equation

(\Gamma_{\text{2R}},\Gamma_{\text{2R}})=2h^{3}(\Gamma_{\text{div}}^{(1)},\Gamma_{1,\text{div}}^{(2)})+O(\hbar^{4}).

(51)

Using mathematical induction, we assume that the effective action
\Gamma_{m-1\,\text{R}}
constructed on the basis
S_{m-1\,\text{R}}
is finite up to terms of order
\hbar^{m-1}
. Then, one finds that the full renormalized action
S_{\text{R}}
given by

S_{\text{R}}=S-\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\hbar^{m}\Gamma_{m-1,\text{div}}^{(m)}

(52)

satisfies exactly the master equation (
38
).
Moreover, the renormalized effective action
\Gamma_{\text{R}}
is finite to each order in perturbation theory:

\Gamma_{\text{R}}=S+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\hbar^{m}\Gamma_{m-1,\text{fin}}^{(m)}

(53)

and satisfies as well the master equation (
38
).

Thus, we have shown that the theory is renormalizable in the sense that all infinites can be eliminated by an appropriate renormalization procedure. The renormalized action
S_{\text{R}}
and the effective action
\Gamma_{\text{R}}
preserve the BRST gauge invariance to all orders in the loop expansion.

V
Gauge invariance of the background effective action

Of special interest is the background effective action
\bar{\Gamma}(\bar{\chi})
defined as the effective action
\Gamma(\bar{\chi},\chi,\chi^{\ddagger})
evaluated for vanishing mean quantum fields and antifields. The Ward identities satisfied by this
action may be obtained in a similar way to that used in Sec. IV, by starting with the generating functional
\bar{Z}(J,\bar{\chi})

\bar{Z}(J,\bar{\chi})=\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi}\exp i\left(S_{\text{FP}}(\chi,\bar{\chi})+\int\mathop{d^{4}x}{J}_{n}\chi_{n}\right),

(54)

where
\bar{\chi}
denotes the background fields
\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
and
\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab}
(see Eq. (
8
)) and
\chi
denotes the quantum fields.

Making the background gauge transformations

\displaystyle{\Delta}\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}={}

\displaystyle-\kappa\epsilon^{\alpha}\bar{q}_{\mu,\alpha}^{a}-\kappa\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}\epsilon^{\alpha}_{,\mu}+\mathchoice{\lambda^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scripts [c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}

(55a)

and

\displaystyle{\Delta}\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab}={}

\displaystyle-\kappa\epsilon^{\alpha}\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab,\alpha}-\bar{\omega}_{\alpha ab}\epsilon^{\alpha}_{,\mu}-\kappa\lambda_{ab,\mu}+\mathchoice{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\bar{\omega}_{\mu pb}+\mathchoice{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}}}_{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\bar{\omega}_{\mu ap},

(55b)

and using the gauge invariance of
\bar{Z}(J,\bar{\chi})
and of
S_{\text{FP}}(\chi,\bar{\chi})
, we obtain that

J_{m}\int\mathop{\mathcal{D}\chi}{\Delta}\chi_{m}\exp i\left(S_{\text{FP}}(\chi,\bar{\chi})+\int\mathop{d^{4}x}J_{n}{\chi}_{n}\right)=0,

(56)

where
\Delta\chi_{m}
denote the corresponding background gauge transformations of the quantum fields. For example,

and

ap}.

(58)

It is now convenient to write the field transformations (
55
) in the compact form used in Sec. III, as

\Delta\chi_{m}=\mathcal{R}_{ml}(\chi)\theta_{l}
,
where
\theta_{1}=\epsilon_{\mu}
and
\theta_{2}=\lambda_{ab}
.
Then, in terms of the generating functional
\bar{W}(J,\bar{\chi})
defined in Eq. (
32
), the Eq. (
56
) becomes

J_{m}\mathcal{R}_{ml}\left(\frac{\delta}{\delta J}\right)\bar{W}(J,\bar{\chi})\theta_{l}=0.

(59)

The background effective action
\bar{\Gamma}(\bar{\chi})
is obtained in the standard way, by using a Legendre transform like
that given in Eq. (
35
). Proceeding in a similar manner to that used previously, we get the result

{\Delta}\bar{\Gamma}(\bar{\chi})={\Delta}\bar{\chi}_{m}\frac{\delta\bar{\Gamma}}{\delta\bar{\chi}_{m}}=0

(60)

which exhibits the diffeomorphism invariance of the background effective action
\bar{\Gamma}(\bar{\chi})
under the background gauge transformations of the background fields
\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
and
\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab}
.

This relation leads to Ward identities that reflect the background gauge symmetry of this action.
Such identities are obtained by taking functional derivatives of Eq. (
60
) with respect to the background fields
\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
and
\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab}
, evaluated at vanishing values of these fields. But taking just a single functional derivative and using the transformations (
55
) of these fields, one gets trivial identities because the tadpoles

\delta\bar{\Gamma}/\delta\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
and
\delta\bar{\Gamma}/\delta\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab}

vanish in our theory.

On the other hand, by taking two functional derivatives of Eq. (
60
) with respect to the background tetrad or spin connection fields, one obtains non-trivial relations which should be satisfied by the corresponding Green functions.
For instance, taking two derivatives with respect to the tetrad fields, it is straightforward to derive the following equations

\partial_{\alpha}\frac{\delta^{2}\bar{\Gamma}}{\delta\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}}\delta\mathchoice{\bar{q}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\bar{q}^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}}\bigg|_{\bar{q}=\bar{\omega}=0}=0,

(61)

where we have used the fact that
\epsilon_{\mu}
is an arbitrary independent parameter.
The Ward identity (
61
) implies that the background tetrad self-energy should be a transverse function.

Figure 1:
One-loop contributions to the self-energy

. The curly lines denote the background tetrad field, while the wavy and double wavy lines are associated, respectively, with the quantum fields

The Feynman diagrams contributing at one loop to the tetrad self-energy are shown in Fig.
1
.
The Feynman rules of the first-order form of the EC theory are derived in any space-time
D
in appendix A.
As shown in appendix C (Eqs. (
148
) and (
149
)), in
D=4-2\epsilon
dimensions, the divergent part of this function has the transverse form

\frac{i\kappa^{2}k^{2}}{16\pi^{2}\epsilon}\left[C_{1}\left(\delta_{a}^{\mu}-\frac{k_{a}k^{\mu}}{k^{2}}\right)\left(\delta_{b}^{\nu}-\frac{k_{b}k^{\nu}}{k^{2}}\right)+C_{2}\left(\delta_{a}^{\nu}-\frac{k_{a}k^{\nu}}{k^{2}}\right)\left(\delta_{b}^{\mu}-\frac{k_{b}k^{\mu}}{k^{2}}\right)+\left(C_{3}\eta_{ab}+C_{5}\frac{k_{a}k_{b}}{k^{2}}\right)\left(\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{k^{\mu}k^{\nu}}{k^{2}}\right)\right],

(62)

where
C_{i}
are linear functions of the gauge parameters
\xi
and
\zeta
introduced in (
11
).

Similarly, the spin connection self-energy is also ultraviolet divergent. It may be verified that this obeys the Ward identity

\partial_{\mu}\frac{\delta^{2}\bar{\Gamma}}{\delta\bar{\omega}_{\mu ab}\delta\bar{\omega}_{\nu cd}}\bigg|_{\bar{q}=\bar{\omega}=0}=0.

(63)

which shows that the spin connection self-energy is also a transverse function.

VI
Discussion

We have examined the renormalizability of the EC theory in the first-order form, where the basic elements are the independent tetrad and spin connection fields. This theory possesses two BRST symmetries, namely, diffeomorphism and local Lorentz invariance. To this end, we used the BV formalism in conjunction with the background field method, which preserves gauge invariance during perturbative renormalization. We have argued that the EC theory in the first-order form is renormalizable, meaning that all the ultraviolet divergences can be subtracted in a gauge-invariant way by local covariant counterterms.

We have studied the background effective action obtained after switching off the quantum fields and antifields. It is important that the background field transformations do not change the form of the gauge-fixing terms for the quantum fields nor that of the ghost terms, which appear in the Faddeev-Popov action. The invariances of the background effective action lead to Ward identities that reflect the gauge symmetries of the first-order form of the EC theory. Such Ward identities relate the Green’s functions and are very useful for simplifying perturbative calculations in this theory (exemplified in the appendices), which are generally quite involved. As an illustration, we have computed the divergent part of the background tetrad self-energy at one-loop order. Apart from the tetrad self-energy, there also occurs a divergent spin-connection as well as mixed tetrad-spin connection self-energy. Upon diagonalizing such a matrix, one would obtain a diagonal self-energy matrix whose elements correspond to the tetrad and spin connection normal modes of the EC theory. This is currently under consideration.

In previous works, it was shown, by using a Lagrange multiplier field that ensures the classical equations of motion, that all contributions beyond one-loop order may be eliminated in quantum gravity. It has also been argued that this important feature leads to a renormalizable and unitary theory
[
27
,
28
]
. It may be worth considering an extension of such an approach to the EC theory, particularly in the formalism recently formulated in
[
29
,
30
]
.

(66)

in the quantum field
\mathchoice{q^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{q^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{q^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{q^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}
.

Integrating out the Nakanishi-Lautrup fields in Eq. (
11
) leads to

\mathcal{L}_{\text{gf}}=-\frac{1}{2\xi}F^{\mu}F_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2\zeta}F^{ab}F_{ab}.

(67)

Thus, we obtain that, in momentum space (
\partial\to+ip
),

\displaystyle A^{\mu\nu}_{ab}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{4\sigma^{2}{p}^{2}\delta^{\mu}_{a}\delta^{\nu}_{b}}{\xi}+\frac{4\sigma{p}_{a}{p}^{\mu}\delta^{\nu}_{b}}{\xi}-\left(\frac{4\sigma{p}_{b}{p}^{\nu}\delta^{\mu}_{a}}{\xi}+\frac{{p}_{a}{p}_{b}\delta^{\mu\nu}}{\xi}+\frac{{p}_{b}{p}^{\mu}\delta^{\nu}_{a}}{\xi}+\frac{{p}_{a}{p}^{\nu}\delta^{\mu}_{b}}{\xi}+\frac{{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}\eta_{ab}}{\xi}\right),

(68a)

\displaystyle B^{\mu\,\rho lm}_{a}={}{}

\displaystyle-i({p}^{l}\delta_{a}^{\mu}\eta^{m\rho}-{p}_{a}\eta^{l\mu}\eta^{m\rho}-{p}^{m}\delta_{a}^{\mu}\eta^{l\rho}-{p}^{l}\eta^{m\mu}\delta_{a}^{\rho}+{p}^{m}\eta^{l\mu}\delta_{a}^{\rho}+{p}_{a}\eta^{m\mu}\eta^{l\rho}),

(68b)

\displaystyle C^{\sigma cd\,\nu}_{b}={}

\displaystyle-B^{\nu\,\sigma cd}_{b},

(68c)

\displaystyle D^{\sigma cd\,\rho lm}={}

(68d)

where
I_{abcd}=(\eta_{ac}\eta_{bd}-\eta_{ad}\eta_{bc})/2
.

In order to derive the propagator, we will invert the
2\times 2
matrix in Eq. (
64
) using

\begin{pmatrix}\bm{A}&\bm{B}\\
\bm{C}&\bm{D}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{X}^{-1}&-\bm{X}^{-1}\bm{B}\bm{D}^{-1}\\
-\bm{D}^{-1}\bm{C}\bm{X}^{-1}&\bm{D}^{-1}+\bm{D}^{-1}\bm{C}\bm{X}\bm{B}\bm{D}^{-1}\end{pmatrix},

(69)

where
\bm{X}=\bm{A}-\bm{B}\bm{D}^{-1}\bm{C}
is the Schur complement.

First, we will compute the inverse of
\bm{D}
. We introduce the tensor basis:

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(1)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta^{ac}\eta^{bd}\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{ad}\eta^{bc}\eta^{\mu\nu}=I^{\mu ab\,\nu cd},

(70a)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(2)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{bd}\eta^{c\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\eta^{ad}\eta^{b\mu}\eta^{c\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{bc}\eta^{d\nu}+\frac{1}{4}\eta^{ac}\eta^{b\mu}\eta^{d\nu},

(70b)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(3)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{bd}\eta^{c\mu}-\frac{1}{4}\eta^{ad}\eta^{b\nu}\eta^{c\mu}-\frac{1}{4}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{bc}\eta^{d\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\eta^{ac}\eta^{b\nu}\eta^{d\mu},

(70c)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(4)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{ac}\eta^{bd}}{2p^{2}}-\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{ad}\eta^{bc}}{2p^{2}},

(70d)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(5)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{p^{c}p^{\mu}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{bd}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{p^{c}p^{\mu}\eta^{ad}\eta^{b\nu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{p^{d}p^{\mu}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{bc}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{p^{d}p^{\mu}\eta^{ac}\eta^{b\nu}}{4p^{2}}

(70e)

\displaystyle-\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{\nu}{g}^{bd}{g}^{c\mu}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{\nu}{g}^{ad}{g}^{c\mu}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{\nu}{g}^{bc}{g}^{d\mu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{\nu}{g}^{ac}{g}^{d\mu}}{4p^{2}},

(70f)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(6)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{p^{c}p^{\nu}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{bd}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{p^{c}p^{\nu}\eta^{ad}\eta^{b\mu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{p^{d}p^{\nu}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{bc}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{p^{d}p^{\nu}\eta^{ac}\eta^{b\mu}}{4p^{2}},

(70g)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(7)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{a}p^{c}\eta^{b\nu}\eta^{d\mu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{p^{b}p^{c}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{d\mu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{p^{a}p^{d}\eta^{b\nu}\eta^{c\mu}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{p^{b}p^{d}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{c\mu}}{4p^{2}},

(70h)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(8)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{a}p^{c}\eta^{b\mu}\eta^{d\nu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{p^{b}p^{c}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{d\nu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{p^{a}p^{d}\eta^{b\mu}\eta^{c\nu}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{p^{b}p^{d}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{c\nu}}{4p^{2}},

(70i)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(9)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{c}{g}^{bd}{g}^{\mu\nu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{c}{g}^{ad}{g}^{\mu\nu}}{4p^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{d}{g}^{bc}{g}^{\mu\nu}}{4p^{2}}+\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{d}{g}^{ac}{g}^{\mu\nu}}{4p^{2}},

(70j)

\displaystyle(T_{QQ}^{(10)})^{\mu ab\,\nu cd}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{a}p^{c}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{bd}}{4p^{4}}-\frac{p^{b}p^{c}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{ad}}{4p^{4}}-\frac{p^{a}p^{d}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{bc}}{4p^{4}}+\frac{p^{b}p^{d}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{ac}}{4p^{4}}.

(70k)

We obtain

(D^{-1})^{\sigma cd\,\rho lm}=\sum_{i=1}^{10}y_{(i)}(T^{(i)}_{QQ})^{\sigma cd\,\rho lm},

(71)

where

\displaystyle y_{(1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle y_{(2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left(\zeta+p^{2}\right)}{(D-2)\zeta+(D-3)p^{2}}

(72a)

\displaystyle y_{(3)}

\displaystyle=-1

\displaystyle y_{(4)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\zeta}{2\zeta+p^{2}}

(72b)

\displaystyle y_{(5)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\zeta}{2\zeta+p^{2}}-1

\displaystyle y_{(6)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2p^{2}}{(D-2)\zeta+(D-3)p^{2}}

(72c)

\displaystyle y_{(7)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\zeta}{2\zeta+p^{2}}-1

\displaystyle y_{(8)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2p^{2}}{(D-2)\left((D-2)\zeta+(D-3)p^{2}\right)}

(72d)

\displaystyle y_{(9)}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2\zeta}{2\zeta+p^{2}}

\displaystyle y_{(10)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2(D-4)p^{4}}{\left(2\zeta+p^{2}\right)\left((D-2)\zeta+(D-3)p^{2}\right)}

(72e)

and
D
is the spacetime dimension.

Then, using Eq. (
68
) and the result in Eq. (
71
), we obtain the Schur complement (for simplicity, in
D=4
):

\begin{split}X^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}&A^{a\mu\,b\nu}-\mathchoice{B^{{{a\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.47081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.33627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.54257pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.95897pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma cd}}}}{B^{{{a\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.47081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.33627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.54257pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.95897pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma cd}}}}{B^{{{a\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.47081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.33627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.54257pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.95897pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma cd}}}}{B^{{{a\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.37056pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.47081pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.33627pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.19997pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.54257pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.95897pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma cd}}}}(D^{-1})^{\sigma cd\rho lm}\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.3941pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.99577pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b\nu}}}_{{{\rho lm}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.52342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.231pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.3941pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.99577pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b\nu}}}_{{{\rho lm}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.52342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.231pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.3941pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.99577pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b\nu}}}_{{{\rho lm}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.52342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.231pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.81456pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.3941pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.99577pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b\nu}}}_{{{\rho lm}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.52594pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.52342pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.231pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\\
={}&-\frac{4\sigma^{2}{p}^{2}{\eta}^{a\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}}{\xi}+\frac{4\sigma{p}^{a}{p}^{\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}}{\xi}+\frac{4\sigma{p}^{b}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{a\mu}}{\xi}-\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{b\mu}}{\xi}-\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{b}{\eta}^{\mu\nu}}{\xi}-\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{\mu}{\eta}^{a\nu}}{\xi}-\frac{{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{ab}}{\xi}\\
&\quad+3{p}^{a}{p}^{b}{\eta}^{\mu\nu}-{p}^{a}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{b\mu}-{p}^{a}{p}^{\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}-{p}^{b}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{a\mu}-{p}^{b}{p}^{\mu}{\eta}^{a\nu}+{p}^{2}{\eta}^{a\nu}{\eta}^{b\mu}+{p}^{2}{\eta}^{a\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}\\
&\quad+\frac{5{p}^{a}{p}^{b}{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}+\frac{2\zeta{p}^{a}{p}^{b}{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}}{{p}^{2}\left({p}^{2}+2\zeta\right)}-\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{b}{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}}{{p}^{2}}\frac{6\zeta{p}^{a}{p}^{\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}-\frac{8\zeta{p}^{a}{p}^{b}{\eta}^{\mu\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}+\frac{6\zeta{p}^{b}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{a\mu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}\\
&\quad-\frac{2\zeta{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{ab}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}+\frac{{p}^{4}{\eta}^{a\nu}{\eta}^{b\mu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}-\frac{3{p}^{4}{\eta}^{a\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}+\frac{3{p}^{2}{p}^{a}{p}^{\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}+\frac{3{p}^{2}{p}^{b}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{a\mu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}+\frac{2\zeta{p}^{2}{\eta}^{ab}{\eta}^{\mu\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}\\
&\quad-\frac{{p}^{2}{p}^{a}{p}^{\nu}{\eta}^{b\mu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}-\frac{{p}^{2}{p}^{b}{p}^{\mu}{\eta}^{a\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}-\frac{6\zeta{p}^{2}{\eta}^{a\mu}{\eta}^{b\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}-\frac{7{p}^{2}{p}^{a}{p}^{b}{\eta}^{\mu\nu}}{{p}^{2}+2\zeta}.\end{split}

(73)

A.2
Propagator
qq

The propagator
(P_{qq})_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\equiv\langle 0|T\mathchoice{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{e^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{e^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{e^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{e^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{e^{{{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}|0\rangle
of the tetrad field is given by
i(\bm{X}^{-1})_{\mu\nu}^{ab}
. To invert
\bm{X}
, we use the basis:

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(1)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{b\nu},

(74a)

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(2)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{b\mu},

(74b)

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(3)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\eta^{ab}\eta^{\mu\nu}\equiv I^{a\mu b\nu},

(74c)

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(4)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}\eta^{ab}}{p^{2}},

(74d)

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(5)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{a}p^{b}\eta^{\mu\nu}}{p^{2}},

(74e)

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(6)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{a}p^{\mu}\eta^{b\nu}}{p^{2}}+\frac{p^{b}p^{\nu}\eta^{a\mu}}{p^{2}},

(74f)

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(7)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{a}p^{\nu}\eta^{b\mu}}{p^{2}}+\frac{p^{b}p^{\mu}\eta^{a\nu}}{p^{2}},

(74g)

\displaystyle{(T_{qq}^{(8)})}^{a\mu\,b\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{p^{a}p^{b}p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{p^{4}}.

(74h)

One can verify that

(\bm{X}^{-1})^{a\mu\,b\nu}=-\sum_{i=1}^{8}x_{(i)}(T_{qq}^{(i)})^{a\mu\,b\nu},

(75)

where

\displaystyle x_{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(D-2)p^{2}}

\displaystyle x_{(2)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\zeta+p^{2}}{2p^{4}}

(76a)

\displaystyle x_{(3)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta}{2p^{4}}

\displaystyle x_{(4)}

\displaystyle=\frac{-4\xi+D(2\xi+1)-3}{2(D-2)p^{2}}

(76b)

\displaystyle x_{(5)}

\displaystyle=\frac{D-3}{2(D-2)p^{2}}

\displaystyle x_{(6)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-2\sigma}{2(D-2)p^{2}(\sigma-1)}

(76c)

\displaystyle x_{(7)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(D-2)p^{2}}

\displaystyle x_{(8)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-2\sigma)(D(\xi(2\sigma-3)-2)+2(\xi+1)(2\sigma-3))}{4(D-2)p^{2}(\sigma-1)^{2}}.

(76d)

We have a pole when
\sigma=1
which is associated to the vanishing of the Faddeev-Popov determinant
[
21
]
.
The gauge (
65a
) and the de Donder gauge are equivalent in the lowest order in
\kappa
. Thus, the propagators in either gauge should be the same.

A.3
Propagators
QQ
,
Qq
and
qQ

We proceed, and compute the
QQ
propagator
P_{QQ}
which is given by
i(\bm{D}^{-1}+\bm{D}^{-1}\bm{C}\bm{X}\bm{B}\bm{D}^{-1})
. We obtain that

(P_{QQ})_{\mu ab\,\nu cd}=i\sum_{i=1}^{10}z_{(i)}(T_{QQ}^{(i)})_{\mu ab\,\nu cd},

(77)

where

\displaystyle z_{(1)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle z_{(2)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2}{D-2}

\displaystyle z_{(3)}={}

\displaystyle-1

\displaystyle z_{(4)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\zeta}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle z_{(5)}={}

\displaystyle-1

(78a)

\displaystyle z_{(6)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{2}{D-2}

\displaystyle z_{(7)}={}

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle z_{(8)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{D-2}

\displaystyle z_{(9)}={}

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle z_{(10)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(D-3)}{D-2}.

(78b)

To compute the mixed propagators, we introduce another basis:

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(1)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{b}\eta^{ac}\eta^{\mu\nu}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{a}\eta^{bc}\eta^{\mu\nu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79a)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(2)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{b}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{c\mu}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{a}\eta^{b\nu}\eta^{c\mu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79b)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(3)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{b}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{c\nu}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{a}\eta^{b\mu}\eta^{c\nu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79c)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(4)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{\nu}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{bc}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{\nu}\eta^{ac}\eta^{b\mu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79d)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(5)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{\mu}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{bc}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{\mu}\eta^{ac}\eta^{b\nu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79e)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(6)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{c}\eta^{a\mu}\eta^{b\nu}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{c}\eta^{a\nu}\eta^{b\mu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79f)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(7)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{c}{p}^{\mu}\eta^{b\nu}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{c}{p}^{\mu}\eta^{a\nu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79g)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(8)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{c}{p}^{\nu}\eta^{b\mu}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{c}{p}^{\nu}\eta^{a\mu}}{2{p}^{2}},

(79h)

\displaystyle(T_{Qq}^{(9)})^{\mu ab\,c\nu}={}

\displaystyle\frac{{p}^{a}{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}\eta^{bc}}{2{p}^{2}}-\frac{{p}^{b}{p}^{\mu}{p}^{\nu}\eta^{ac}}{2{p}^{2}}.

(79i)

The
Qq
propagator
P_{Qq}
is given by
-i\bm{D}^{-1}\bm{C}\bm{X}^{-1}
. Computing and projecting it in basis (
79
) yields

(P_{Qq})_{\mu ab\,c\nu}=i\sum_{i=1}^{9}w_{(i)}(T_{Qq}^{(i)})_{\mu ab\,c\nu},

(80)

where

\displaystyle w_{(1)}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle w_{(2)}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle w_{(3)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2-D}

(81a)

\displaystyle w_{(4)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2-D}

\displaystyle w_{(5)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\zeta}{p^{2}}

\displaystyle w_{(6)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{D-2}

(81b)

\displaystyle w_{(7)}

\displaystyle=\frac{D-3}{(D-2)p^{2}}

\displaystyle w_{(8)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-2\sigma}{(D-2)(\sigma-1)p^{2}}

\displaystyle w_{(9)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(D-2)p^{2}}

(81c)

One can verify that
(P_{qQ})_{c\nu\,\mu ab}=-(P_{Qq})_{\mu ab\,c\nu}
. Note that, the propagators
QQ
,
Qq
and
qQ
have no dependency on the gauge parameter
\xi
because

\bm{C}\bm{X}^{-1}\bm{A}=0.

(82)

A.4
Ghost sector

The bilinear terms in
{c}^{\star}_{\mu}
,
c_{\mu}
and
{C}^{\star}_{ab}
,
C_{ab}
from the ghost Lagrangian (
12
) reads

\begin{split}&-c^{\star\nu}\eta_{ab}\big\{\left[\delta_{\mu}^{a}\left(\delta_{\alpha}^{b}c_{,\bar{\nu}}^{\alpha}\right)+\delta_{\nu}^{b}\left(\delta_{\alpha}^{a}c_{,\bar{\mu}}^{\alpha}\right)\right]^{,\bar{\mu}}-2\sigma[\delta_{\mu}^{a}(\delta_{\alpha}^{b}c^{\alpha,\bar{\mu}})]_{,\bar{\nu}}\big\}\\

ab}\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.00659pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.43326pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{,\bar{\mu}}}}_{{{ab,\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.36113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.97223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.00659pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.43326pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{,\bar{\mu}}}}_{{{ab,\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.36113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.97223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.00659pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.43326pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{,\bar{\mu}}}}_{{{ab,\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.36113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.97223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[16.34627pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.00659pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.43326pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{,\bar{\mu}}}}_{{{ab,\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.26392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.36113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.97223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}.\end{split}

(83)

However, note that the second term in above equation can be simplified to

c^{\star\nu}\big\{\left[\delta_{\mu}^{a}\left(\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0554pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ap}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0554pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ap}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0554pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ap}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0554pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ap}}}}\delta_{\nu}^{p}\right)+\delta_{\nu}^{b}\left(\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56822pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.26302pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bp}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56822pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.26302pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bp}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56822pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.26302pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bp}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56822pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.26302pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{bp}}}}\delta_{\mu}^{p}\right)\right]^{,\bar{\mu}}-2\sigma\left[\delta_{\mu}^{a}\left(\mathchoice{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.0554pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.611pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ap}}}}{C^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.45999pt][c]{$\textsty (84)

which vanishes, since
C_{ab}=-C_{ba}
. Then, we see that, in this order, the ghost fields decouple. There is no mixed ghost propagators.

Using Eq. (
84
) in Eq. (
83
), we have

\begin{split}&-c^{\star\nu}\big[\eta_{\mu\nu}\partial^{\pi}\partial_{\pi}+(1-2\sigma)\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}\big]c^{\mu}-C^{\star ab}\partial_{\pi}\partial^{\pi}I_{ablm}\mathchoice{C^{{{lm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86073pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18623pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{{lm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86073pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18623pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{{lm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86073pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18623pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{C^{{{lm}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.66557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86073pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18623pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}.\end{split}

(85)

The propagator
(P_{{C}^{\star}C})_{ablm}\equiv\left\langle{C}^{\star}_{ab}C_{lm}\right\rangle
of the ghost field
{C}_{ab}
is simply given by

-\frac{i}{p^{2}}I_{ablm}.

(86)

While the propagator
(P_{{c}^{\star}c})_{\mu\nu}\equiv\left\langle{c}^{\star}_{\mu}c_{\nu}\right\rangle
is

-\frac{i}{p^{2}}\left(\eta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-2\sigma}{\sigma-1}\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}}\right).

(87)

When
\sigma=1/2
(in de Donder-like gauge), it simplifies to
-i\eta_{\mu\nu}/p^{2}
.

A.5
Vertices

To derive the vertices, we need to consider the expansion (
8
) in several terms. In the determinant
e
, we obtain that

e=\bar{e}\left[1+\frac{\kappa}{2}\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+O(\kappa^{2})\right],

(88)

where

(89)

(90)

We have omitted nonlinear terms on the quantum field

.
